### Ecclesiastical Titles

# Hispania

d_et_toledo = { # Toledo

	}

	800.1.1 = { holder = MB_samarkand_isoyahb } # Isoyahb; invented, Timothy I (780-823) consecrated a metropolitan for Beth Turkaye, possibly the same province as Samarkand
	842.1.1 = { holder = MB_samarkand_pethion } # Pethion; invented
	884.1.1 = { holder = MB_samarkand_hnanisho } # Hnanisho; invented, Eliya of Damascus lists Qand (Samarkand) as a metropolitan province in 893
	926.1.1 = { holder = MB_samarkand_shemon } # Shemon; invented
	968.1.1 = { holder = MB_samarkand_giwargis } # Giwargis; invented
	1005.1.1 = { holder = MB_samarkand_yohannan } # Yohannan; invented, a metropolitan of Samarkand is attested in 1018 but not named
	1048.1.1 = { holder = MB_samarkand_dawid } # Dawid; invented
	1090.1.1 = { holder = MB_samarkand_sabrisho } # Sabrisho; invented
	1132.1.1 = { holder = MB_samarkand_eliya } # Eliya; invented
	1174.1.1 = { holder = MB_samarkand_abdisho } # Abdisho; invented
	1216.1.1 = { holder = MB_samarkand_yaqob } # Yaqob; invented, Samarkand surrendered to Genghis Khan in 1220 and was not destroyed
	1258.1.1 = { holder = MB_samarkand_sliba } # Sliba; invented, Marco Polo mentions an East Syriac community in Samarkand in the 1270s
	1300.1.1 = { holder = MB_samarkand_gabriel } # Gabriel; invented, covers past 1337, few Christian graves postdate the 1340s so death estimated 1345
	1345.1.2 = { holder = 0 } # defunct; Central Asian Christian communities faded in the mid-14th century
}

d_et_kashgar = { # Kashgar and Navekath - East Syriac metropolitan province of Central Asia (Kashgar in the Tarim basin and Navekath in Semirechye), created by Patriarch Eliya III (1176-90). No holder is named in the sources, so every holder is invented. The province is treated as ending in 1342, the date of the last dated East Syriac tombstones in Central Asia
	769.1.1 = {
		government = ecclesiastical_government
		clerical_region = et_867_kashgar_region
	}
	1180.1.1 = {
		holder = MB_kashgar_yohannan # Yohannan; invented, first metropolitan after Patriarch Eliya III (1176-90) created the metropolitan province of Kashgar and Navekath; start date approximate
		government = ecclesiastical_government
		clerical_region = et_1187_kashgar_region
	}
	1220.1.1 = { holder = MB_kashgar_mari } # Mari; invented, Kashgar and Navekath pass from Qara Khitai to Mongol rule in these years
	1240.1.1 = {
		clerical_region = et_1246_kashgar_region 
	}
	1260.1.1 = { holder = MB_kashgar_sargis } # Sargis; invented
	1300.1.1 = { holder = MB_kashgar_eliya } # Eliya; invented, covers past 1337, the last dated East Syriac tombstones in Central Asia are from 1342
	1342.1.2 = { holder = 0 } # defunct; Central Asian East Syriac communities ended by the 1340s
}

d_et_china = { # China - East Syriac metropolitan province of Beth Sinaye (China), created by Patriarch Sliba-zkha (714-28), known from the Xi'an Stele of 781 and Dawid of Beth Abe under Timothy I, and damaged by the 845 edict, the 878/9 Guangzhou massacre and the fall of the Tang in 907. The province is treated as lapsing in 907 and revived in 1281 as Katai and Ong (northern China and the Ongud, seat probably at Khanbaliq), which seems to have replaced it, ending with the fall of the Yuan in 1368. The invented holders fill unattested gaps
	720.1.1 = {
		holder = MB_china_sabrisho # Sabrisho; invented, first metropolitan of Beth Sinaye after Patriarch Sliba-zkha (714-28) created the province
		government = ecclesiastical_government
		clerical_region = et_769_china_region
		effect = {
			set_capital_county = title:c_jingzhao
			title_domicile ?= {
				move_domicile = title:c_jingzhao.title_province
			}
		}
	} # capital at Chang'an (c_jingzhao), where Adam wrote the 781 stele
	758.1.1 = { holder = MB_china_adam } # Adam (Jingjing), priest, bishop and papash of Sinistan who composed the Xi'an Stele inscription of 781, probably metropolitan of Beth Sinaye; dates of tenure estimated
	797.1.1 = { holder = MB_china_dawid } # Dawid, monk of Beth Abe, elected metropolitan of Beth Sinaye under Timothy I (780-823) after the death of the previous metropolitan; dates of tenure estimated
	836.1.1 = { holder = MB_china_hormizd } # Hormizd; invented, tenure spans the 845 edict of Emperor Wuzong against foreign religions
	872.1.1 = { holder = MB_china_yaqob } # Yaqob; invented, tenure spans the 878/9 Guangzhou massacre and the fall of the Tang in 907
	907.1.2 = { holder = 0 } # lapsed with the fall of the Tang; China is last mentioned as a province in 987, when Christianity there was reported extinct
	1281.1.1 = {
		holder = MB_china_yahballaha_iii # Markos, Yahballaha III, consecrated metropolitan of Katai and Ong by Patriarch Denha I shortly before Denha's death in 1281; elected patriarch in November 1281
		clerical_region = et_1282_katai_ong_region
		name = KATAIONG
		effect = {
			set_capital_county = title:c_youzhou_china
			title_domicile ?= {
				move_domicile = title:c_youzhou_china.title_province
			}
		}
	} # capital moves to Khanbaliq (c_youzhou_china), where the metropolitans of Katai and Ong probably sat
	1281.11.21 = { holder = MB_china_giwargis } # Giwargis, metropolitan named in the biography of Yahballaha III, here placed as his successor in Katai and Ong; dates of tenure estimated
	1305.1.1 = { holder = MB_china_nestoris } # Nestoris, metropolitan named in the biography of Yahballaha III, here placed as successor to Giwargis in Katai and Ong; dates of tenure estimated, covers past 1337
	1350.1.1 = { holder = MB_china_yohannan } # Yohannan; invented, last metropolitan, in office until the fall of the Yuan in 1368 and covers past 1337
	1368.1.2 = { holder = 0 } # defunct; the Yuan fell in 1368 and foreign Christians were likely expelled
}

d_et_tangut = { # Tangut - East Syriac metropolitan province of north-western China, with its metropolitan probably seated at Almaliq. Sources date the province to the end of the 13th century, but a metropolitan already existed in 1281, so it is placed earlier under Mongol rule. The only named metropolitan is Shemon bar Qaligh (1281); the other holders are invented. The province is treated as ending in 1339-40, when the Christians of Almaliq were forcibly converted to Islam
	1240.1.1 = {
		holder = MB_tangut_sargis # Sargis; invented, first metropolitan of Tangut, placed in the years after the Mongol conquest of the Tangut state in 1227; the true creation date is unknown and is only known to be before 1281
		government = ecclesiastical_government
		clerical_region = et_1282_tangut_region
	}
	1281.1.1 = { holder = MB_tangut_shemon } # Shemon bar Qaligh, metropolitan of Tangut, arrested with a number of his bishops by Patriarch Denha I shortly before Denha's death in 1281 and present at the consecration of Yahballaha III later that year; start of tenure set to 1281 and end estimated
	1315.1.1 = { holder = MB_tangut_mattai } # Mattai; invented, covers past 1337, the Christians of Almaliq were forcibly converted in 1339/40
	1340.1.2 = { holder = 0 } # defunct; the Christians of Almaliq were forcibly converted in 1339/40 and no later metropolitan is known
}

d_et_hamadan = { # Hulwan / Hamadan - East Syriac metropolitan province of Media (Beth Madaye), created under Patriarch Ishoyahb II (628-45) with its metropolitans seated at Hulwan, listed by Eliya of Damascus in 893 with Dinawar, Hamadan, Nihawand and al-Kuj as suffragans. Named metropolitans are Surin (751/2), Ishoyahb of Susa (893), Ishodad (938), Yohannan (961-87), Mari (1018/19), Abd al-Masih (1075), Yohannan (1092-1111) and a last Yohannan (1176-90). The seat moved from Hulwan to Hamadan around 1200 and the title is treated as ending with the Mongol sack of Hamadan in 1220. The invented holders fill unattested gaps
	745.1.1 = {
		holder = MB_hamadan_surin # Surin, metropolitan of Hulwan in 751/2 and irregularly consecrated patriarch (Elijah of Nisibis); start and end of tenure estimated
		government = ecclesiastical_government
		clerical_region = et_769_hamadan_region
	}
	754.1.1 = { holder = MB_hamadan_hnanisho } # Hnanisho; invented
	790.1.1 = { holder = MB_hamadan_narsai } # Narsai; invented
	825.1.1 = { holder = MB_hamadan_shahrdost } # Shahrdost; invented
	860.1.1 = { holder = MB_hamadan_giwargis } # Giwargis; invented
	893.7.15 = { holder = MB_hamadan_ishoyahb } # Ishoyahb of Susa, appointed metropolitan of Hulwan by Patriarch Yohannan III right after his consecration on 15 July 893; end of tenure estimated
	925.1.1 = { holder = MB_hamadan_ishodad } # Ishodad, metropolitan of Hulwan at the consecration of Patriarch Emmanuel I on 23 February 938 (Eliya of Nisibis); start and end of tenure estimated
	957.1.1 = { holder = MB_hamadan_yohannan_i } # Yohannan, metropolitan of Hulwan at the consecrations of Israel (961) and Mari (987), probably the same man (Mari, Sliba); start and end of tenure estimated
	1004.1.1 = { holder = MB_hamadan_mari } # Mari "of Fars", formerly bishop of Dinawar, metropolitan in the city of Hulwan when Elijah of Nisibis finished his Chronography in 1018/19; start and end of tenure estimated
	1040.1.1 = { holder = MB_hamadan_mihran } # Mihran; invented
	1072.1.1 = { holder = MB_rai_abd_al_masih } # Abd al-Masih, metropolitan of Hulwan and Rai at the consecration of Abdisho II in 1075 (Mari); also holds Rai (d_et_rai), whose seat seems to have moved to Hulwan; start estimated, died during that patriarchate
	1085.1.1 = { holder = MB_hamadan_yohannan_ii } # Yohannan, metropolitan of Hulwan at the consecrations of Makkikha I (1092) and Eliya II (1111), possibly the same man (Mari); start and end of tenure estimated
	1118.1.1 = { holder = MB_hamadan_ishaq } # Ishaq; invented, stands for the unnamed metropolitan of Hulwan who was transferred to the metropolitan diocese of Egypt under Patriarch Eliya II (1111-32) (Mari)
	1130.1.1 = { holder = MB_hamadan_sliba } # Sliba; invented
	1154.1.1 = { holder = MB_hamadan_eliya } # Eliya; invented
	1176.1.1 = { holder = MB_hamadan_yohannan_iii } # Yohannan, last known bishop of Hulwan and Hamadan, in office under Patriarch Eliya III (1176-90); start and end of tenure estimated
	1195.1.1 = { holder = MB_hamadan_abraham } # Abraham; invented, the metropolitan see moved from Hulwan to Hamadan around the beginning of the 13th century
	1221.1.2 = { holder = 0 } # defunct after the Mongol sack of Hamadan in 1220; no later metropolitan is known
}

d_et_maishan = { # Maishan (Basra) - fourth-ranking East Syriac metropolitan province, seat at Prath d'Maishan (Basra); the last known metropolitan is Shlemun in 1222 and the province seems to have ended in the 13th century. Only the named metropolitans are attested, the rest are invented
	745.1.1 = {

	833.1.1 = { holder = MB_maishan_shuhalmaran } # Shuhalmaran, died three months after the consecration of Patriarch Abraham II in 837; start estimated
	838.1.1 = { holder = MB_maishan_daniel } # Daniel, took part in the consecration of Patriarch Theodosius in 853; end estimated
	860.1.1 = { holder = MB_maishan_ishodnah } # Isho'dnah of Basra, author of the Book of Chastity and a lost history, probably active in the 860s-870s; start and end estimated
	880.1.1 = { holder = MB_maishan_gabriel } # Gabriel of Basra, compiler of the first Syriac nomocanon, attested 884 and 893; start and end estimated
	905.1.1 = { holder = MB_maishan_sabrisho } # Sabrisho; invented
	945.1.1 = { holder = MB_maishan_abd_al_masih } # Abd al-Masih of Basra, one of three metropolitans at the consecration of Patriarch Israel in 961, d. 986; start estimated
	986.1.2 = { holder = 0 } # see vacant until Joseph
	987.1.1 = { holder = MB_maishan_joseph } # Joseph of al-Hira, appointed by Patriarch Mari (987-999) and died before Abraham of Shahrzur; end estimated
	992.1.1 = { holder = MB_maishan_abraham } # Abraham of Shahrzur, appointed by Mari after Joseph's death; start and end estimated
	1025.1.1 = { holder = MB_maishan_yohannan_ii } # Yohannan; invented, the unnamed metropolitan of Basra at the consecration of Patriarch Eliya I in 1028
	1064.1.1 = { holder = MB_maishan_giwargis } # Giwargis, a monk consecrated by Patriarch Sabrisho III c.1063/4, styled "Basra and Prath Maishan" in 1074 and died in the reign of Abdisho II (1074-1090); end estimated

	1200.1.1 = { holder = MB_maishan_shlemun } # Shlemun (Solomon) of Maishan, author of the Book of the Bee, present at the consecration of Patriarch Sabrisho IV in 1222, the last known metropolitan; start and end estimated
	1245.1.2 = { holder = 0 } # defunct; no metropolitan of Maishan is known after Shlemun
}
